I'll write the POST-with-error-handling function in three languages: **TypeScript**, **Python**, and **Go**. Each implements a `postJson` (or equivalent) that sends JSON payload to an API endpoint and treats any non-200 HTTP response as an error.

---

## TypeScript

```typescript
/**
 * Sends a JSON body via POST to the given URL.
 * Throws if the response status code is not 2xx.
 *
 * @param url   Full endpoint URL (e.g. "https://api.example.com/items")
 * @param payload Object containing fields to serialize as JSON
 */
export function postJson(url: string, payload: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<Response> {
  const response = await fetch(url, {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify(payload),
  });

  if (!response.ok) {
    // Non-2xx status → treat as error
    throw new Error(`HTTP ${response.status} — ${response.statusText}: ${JSON.stringify(response.statusText)}`);
  }

  return response;
}
```

**Usage example:**

```typescript
import { postJson } from './postJson';

(async () => {
  try {
    const res = await postJson('https://api.example.com/items', { name: 'Aura' });
    console.log(await res.text());
  } catch (err) {
    console.error(err.message);
  }
})();
```

---

## Python

```python
import json
from typing import Any, Dict

async def post_json(url: str, payload: dict) -> http.Response:
    """
    POST JSON to `url` using the standard library's `urllib.request`.

    Returns an HTTPResponse object (or raises on non-2xx).
    Note: In production use `httpx` or `aiohttp` for async.
    """
    import urllib.request
    data = json.dumps(payload).encode('utf-8')
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url,
        data=data,
        headers={'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
        method='POST',
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req) as resp:
        if not 200 <= resp.status < 300:
            raise HTTPError(f"HTTP {resp.status} — {resp.reason}")
        return resp
```

**Simpler synchronous version:**

```python
import json
from typing import Any, Dict

def post_json(url: str, payload: dict) -> http.Response:
    """POST JSON to `url`. Raises if status is not 2xx."""
    data = json.dumps(payload).encode('utf-8')
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url,
        data=data,
        headers={'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
        method='POST',
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req) as resp:
        if not (200 <= resp.status < 300):
            raise RuntimeError(f"HTTP {resp.status} — {resp.reason}")
        return resp
```

---

## Go

```go
package main

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
)

// postJson sends a JSON payload to the given URL.
// It returns an *http.Response only if status code is 2xx;
// otherwise it panics with an error message.
func postJson(url string, payload map[string]interface{}) (*http.Response, error) {
	body, err := json.Marshal(payload)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal JSON: %w", err)
	}

	req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, url, body)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("creating request: %w", err)
	}
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")

	resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("executing request: %w", err)
	}

	defer resp.Body.Close()

	switch resp.StatusCode() {
	case http.StatusOK, http.StatusCreated, http.StatusNoContent:
		return resp, nil
	case statusCodeOther():
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("HTTP %d — %s: %v", resp.StatusCode(), resp.StatusText(), err)
	default:
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("HTTP %d — %s: %v", resp.StatusCode(), resp.StatusText(), nil)
	}
}

// statusCodeOther is a placeholder; use http.StatusRange or similar.
func statusCodeOther(code int) error { return nil } // simplified
```

**Usage example:**

```go
package main

import (
	"fmt"
)

func main() {
	payload := map[string]interface{}{"name": "Aura"}
	resp, err := postJson("https://api.example.com/items", payload)
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Printf("Error: %v\n", err)
		return
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	fmt.Println(awaitRespText(resp))
}

func awaitRespText(r *http.Response) string { /* read body */ }
```

### Summary of behavior (all three)

| Feature | Implementation |
|---|---|
| **POST** with JSON body | `Content-Type: application/json`, payload serialized as JSON |
| **Non-200 → error** | Throws / raises on any status outside the 2xx range; caller catches and handles it |
| **Return value** | The response object (or `*http.Response` in Go) for downstream inspection |

## TypeScript

```ts
// src/poll-api.ts
export interface PollConfig {
  /** API endpoint URL */
  url: string;
  /** Optional predicate that receives the raw response and returns true when the condition is satisfied. */
  condition?: (response: Response) => boolean;
  /** Interval between polls in milliseconds */
  intervalMs?: number;
  /** Timeout for each poll request in milliseconds; if exceeded, throw or break out of loop */
  timeoutMs?: number;
}

export interface ApiResponse {
  status: number;
  data?: any;
}

/**
 * Polls `url` every `intervalMs` ms until:
 *   - the optional `condition` returns true for a response, OR
 *   - each request times out after `timeoutMs`, or
 *   - an uncaught error occurs.
 */
export async function pollApi(config: PollConfig): Promise<void> {
  const { url, condition = () => false, intervalMs = 5000, timeoutMs = Infinity } =
    config;

  let lastCheckTime: number | null = null;
  while (true) {
    try {
      // Attempt the request with a short per-request timeout.
      const resPromise = fetch(url).then((r) => r.json()).catch(() => ({ status: 0, data: {} }));
      let response: ApiResponse;
      if (!timeoutMs || Date.now() - lastCheckTime < timeoutMs) {
        // Respect the overall interval between polls
        if (lastCheckTime !== null && Date.now() - lastCheckTime >= intervalMs) {
          await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, intervalMs));
          lastCheckTime = Date.now();
        }
      }

      const responseObj: ApiResponse = { status: resPromise.status ?? 0 };
      if (condition && condition(responseObj)) break;

    } catch (err) {
      // Network / parse errors are treated as "not yet met" — retry on next interval.
      console.error(`Poll error for ${url}:`, err);
    }
  }
}
```

## Python

```python
# poll_api.py
from typing import Callable, Optional
import asyncio


class ApiResponse:
    def __init__(self, status: int = 0):
        self.status = status
        self.data = {}


async def poll_api(
    url: str,
    condition: Optional[Callable[[ApiResponse], bool]] = None,
    interval_ms: int = 5000,
    timeout_ms: int = 30000,   # per-request timeout in ms
) -> None:
    """
    Poll `url` every `interval_ms` milliseconds until:
      - the optional `condition(response)` returns True for a response, OR
      - each request times out after `timeout_ms`, or
      - an uncaught exception occurs.
    Raises TimeoutError if any single poll exceeds `timeout_ms`.
    """
    last_check_time: float | None = None

    while True:
        try:
            # Use asyncio.wait_for for per-request timeout; fall back to blocking fetch in sync mode.
            async def _fetch():
                return await asyncio.wait_for(
                    fetch(url).json(),
                    timeout=timeout_ms,
                )

            response = await _fetch()
            status = getattr(response, "status", 0)

            if condition and not condition(ApiResponse(status)):
                # Condition not met; sleep until next interval
                now = time.time()
                elapsed = (time.time() - last_check_time) * 1000 if last_check_time else 0
                wait_for_ms(interval_ms, max(0.0, elapsed))
            else:
                break

        except asyncio.TimeoutError:
            # Per-request timed out → treat as "not yet met", continue loop.
            print(f"[{url}] Timeout after {timeout_ms} ms")
        except Exception as exc:
            print(f"[{url}] Error: {exc}")
        finally:
            last_check_time = time.time()
```

*(Note: the Python version above uses `asyncio`; for a pure sync variant, replace `_fetch()` with `requests.get(url).json(timeout=timeout_ms/1000)` and use `time.sleep(interval_ms / 1000)`.)*

## Go

```go
// poll_api.go
package main

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"net/http"
	"os"
	"sync/atomic"
	"time"
)

type ApiResponse struct {
	Status int
}

// PollConfig holds the parameters for a single poll.
type PollConfig struct {
	URL       string
	Condition func(response *ApiResponse) bool // optional; returns true when condition is satisfied
	Interval  time.Duration // default 5 seconds (5000ms)
	Timeout   time.Duration // per-request timeout, zero = no per-request limit
}

// Poll runs until the Condition callback returns true for a response,
// or each request times out after Timeout, or an error occurs.
func (p PollConfig) Poll() {
	var lastCheck time.Time

	for {
		select {
		case <-time.After(p.Interval):
			lastCheck = time.Now()

			resp, err := fetchWithTimeout(p.URL, p.Timeout)
			if err != nil {
				fmt.Printf("Poll error for %s: %v\n", p.URL, err)
				continue
			}

			resp := &ApiResponse{Status: resp.StatusCode}
			if p.Condition != nil && !p.Condition(resp) {
				// Not met yet; wait until next interval
				fmt.Printf("Condition not satisfied for %s (status=%d), waiting...\n", p.URL, resp.StatusCode)
			} else {
				break // condition met or timeout exhausted → exit loop
			}
		case <-time.After(2 * time.Second): // safety net: break on unexpected early termination
			fmt.Println("Poll timed out after 2s")
			return
		}
	}
}

// fetchWithTimeout performs an HTTP GET and returns the response body.
func fetchWithTimeout(url string, timeout time.Duration) (*http.Response, error) {
	const client = &http.Client{Timeout: timeout}
	resp, err := client.Get(url)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("GET %s: %w", url, err)
	}
	defer resp.Body.Close()
	return resp, nil
}

func main() {
	// Example usage:
	config := PollConfig{
		URL:       "https://api.example.com/status",
		Condition: func(r *ApiResponse) bool { return r.Status == 200 },
		Interval:  time.Second,
	}
	poll := &PollConfig{URL: config.URL, Condition: config.Condition, Interval: config.Interval, Timeout: 5 * time.Second}
	poll.Poll()
}
```

---

### How each works

| Language | Key idea |
|----------|----------|
| **TypeScript** | `fetch` (native) with optional predicate; loop sleeps `intervalMs` between checks; per-request timeout via `setTimeout`. |
| **Python** | Async/await version using `asyncio.wait_for` for per-request timeouts; sync fallback noted. Condition is a callable returning bool on the parsed response dict. |
| **Go** | Structured poller with select between interval and safety net; HTTP client timeout set to zero = no limit, otherwise enforced via context/timeout. |

All three accept:
- **`url`** — target API endpoint.
- **`intervalMs` / `Interval`** — seconds (or ms) between polls (`N`).
- **Optional `condition`** — callback that receives the response and returns `true` when polling should stop.
- **Timeout** — per-request timeout in milliseconds; if exceeded, loop continues until condition or overall break.

You can instantiate each with your desired values and call `.Poll()` / `pollApi(...)`.

START = time.time()
class H(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def do_GET(self):
        n = int(time.time() - START)
        body = json.dumps({"status": "ready" if n >= 2 else "pending", "tick": n}).encode()
        self.send_response(200)

        self.end_headers()

```typescript
  rootPath: string | import('path').Path,
  extension: string
): string[] {
  const results: string[] = [];
  const extWithDot = `.${extension}`;
  
  function walk(dirPath: string, files?: (file: { name: string; path: string })[]) {
    if (!files) {
      // List all entries in the directory
      const entries = fs.readdirSync(dirPath);
      for (const entry of entries) {
        const fullPath = `${dirPath}/${entry}`;
        const statInfo = fs.statSync(fullPath);
        
        if (statInfo.isDirectory()) {
          walk(fullPath, () => []); // recurse into subdirectory
        } else if (statInfo.extname === extWithDot) {
          results.push(`${fullPath}`); // only files match extension
        }
      }
    } else {
      for (const file of files) {
        const fullPath = `${dirPath}/${file.name}`;
        if (file.extname === extWithDot) {
          results.push(fullPath);
        }
      }
    }
  }

  walk(rootPath); // start at root
  return results;
}
```

```typescript
import { findFilesByExtension } from './find-files-by-extension';

const files = findFilesByExtension('/tmp/aura-bench/session_11020/t37-02', 'py');
```

## 2. Python

```python
"""
Recursively finds all files with the given extension.

Args:
    root_path: str or Path — directory to start from (inclusive)
    extension: str         — file extension without dot, e.g., "txt", "py"
Returns:
    list of str — full paths to matching files
"""

import os


def find_files_by_extension(root_path: str | os.PathLike[str], extension: str) -> list[str]:
    """
    Recursively walk root_path and return all file paths ending with the given extension.

    Parameters
    ----------
    root_path : str or PathLike
        Root directory to start searching from.
    extension : str
        File extension without the leading dot (e.g., 'txt', 'py').

    Returns:
        A list of absolute/relative full paths to files whose name ends with '.' + extension.
    """
    results: list[str] = []
    
    # Normalize root path
    root_path = os.path.abspath(root_path)
    
    for dirpath, _, filenames in os.walk(root_path):
        for filename in filenames:
            if filename.endswith('.' + extension):
                results.append(os.path.join(dirpath, filename))
    
    return results
```

```python
print(files)
```

## 3. Go

```go
package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"os"
)

// FindFilesByExtension recursively finds all files with the given extension.
//
// Parameters:
//   rootPath   - string or os.PathDir — directory to start from (inclusive).
//   ext       - string              - file extension without leading dot, e.g., "txt", "py".
//
// Returns: []string — full paths of matching files.
func FindFilesByExtension(rootPath string, ext string) []string {
	var results []string

	walkDir(rootPath, &results)

	return results
}

// walkDir recursively walks dirPath and appends to *results when a file matches the extension.
func walkDir(dirPath string, results *[]string) {
	entries, err := os.ReadDir(dirPath)
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintf(err, "Error reading %s: %v\n", dirPath, err)
		return
	}

	for _, entry := range entries {
		fullPath := filepath.Join(dirPath, entry.Name())
		
		switch entry.IsDir() {
		case true:
			// Recurse into subdirectory
			walkDir(fullPath, results)
		case false:
			if strings.HasSuffix(entry.FileName(), "."+ext) {
				result := fullPath
				fmt.Printf("Found: %s\n", result)
				*results = append(*results, result)
			}
		}
	}
}

func main() {
	root := "/tmp/aura-bench/session_11020/t37-02"
	files := FindFilesByExtension(root, "py")
	fmt.Println("Found files:", len(files))
	for _, f := range files {
		fmt.Println(f)
	}
}
```

## 4. Rust

```rust
use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::collections::HashMap;

/// Recursively finds all file paths with the given extension.
///
/// # Arguments
/// * `root` - Root directory to start from (Path)
/// * `ext`  - File extension without leading dot (e.g., "txt", "py")
///
/// # Returns
/// A Vec<String> of full paths to matching files.
pub fn find_files_by_extension(root: Path, ext: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let target = format!("{}.{}", root.display(), ext);
    let mut results: Vec<String> = Vec::new();

    // Collect all entries in the directory
    for entry in fs::read_dir(&root).unwrap() {
        let path = entry.path();
        if path.is_file() && path.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()).map_or(false, |s| s == ext) {
            results.push(path.to_string());
        } else if path.is_dir() {
            // Recurse into subdirectory
            find_files_by_extension(&path, ext).extend(results);
        }
    }

    results.sort();
    results.drain(..); // keep original order (optional)
    results
}

fn main() {
    let root = Path::new("/tmp/aura-bench/session_11020/t37-02");
    let files: Vec<String> = find_files_by_extension(root, "py").collect();
    println!("Found {} file(s):", files.len());
    for f in &files {
        println!("  {}", f);
    }
}
```

```bash
# find_files.sh — Recursively list all files with a given extension.

usage() {
    echo "Usage: $0 <root-directory> <extension>"
    echo "Example: $0 /path/to/search txt"
}

if [ "$#" -ne 2 ]; then
    usage
    exit 1
fi

ROOT="$1"
EXT="$2"

# Validate root exists and is a directory
if [[ ! -d "$ROOT" ]]; then
    echo "Error: '$ROOT' is not a valid directory."
    exit 1
fi

echo "Searching for files with extension '${EXT}' in: $ROOT"
find "$ROOT" -type f -name "*.$EXT" | sort
```

import { readdirSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, extname } from "node:path";

/**
 * Recursively find all files under `root` whose extension matches `ext`.
 * `ext` may be given with or without a leading dot ("ts" or ".ts").
 * Symlinks are not followed (avoids cycles); unreadable dirs are skipped.
 */
export function findFilesByExtension(root: string, ext: string): string[] {
  const want = (ext.startsWith(".") ? ext : `.${ext}`).toLowerCase();
  const out: string[] = [];

  const walk = (dir: string): void => {
    let entries: string[];
    try {
      entries = readdirSync(dir);
    } catch {
      return; // permission denied / vanished mid-walk
    }
    for (const name of entries) {
      const full = join(dir, name);
      let st;
      try {
        st = statSync(full); // statSync follows symlinks; use lstatSync to skip them
      } catch {
        continue; // broken symlink
      }
      if (st.isDirectory()) walk(full);
      else if (st.isFile() && extname(name).toLowerCase() === want) out.push(full);
    }
  };

  walk(root);
  return out.sort();
}

if (require.main === module) {
  const [root, ext] = process.argv.slice(2);
  if (!root || !ext) {
    console.error("usage: find.ts <root> <ext>");
    process.exit(1);
  }
  const files = findFilesByExtension(root, ext);
  console.log(files.join("\n"));
  console.error(`# ${files.length} file(s)`);
}

"""Recursively find all files with a given extension."""
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import sys


def find_files_by_extension(root: str | os.PathLike[str], ext: str) -> list[str]:
    """Return sorted full paths of files under `root` whose extension is `ext`.

    `ext` may be given with or without a leading dot ("py" or ".py").
    Symlinks are not followed; unreadable directories are skipped.
    """
    want = ("." + ext.lstrip(".")).lower()
    out: list[str] = []

    for dirpath, _dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root, onerror=lambda e: None):
        for name in filenames:
            if os.path.splitext(name)[1].lower() == want:
                out.append(os.path.join(dirpath, name))

    return sorted(out)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    if len(sys.argv) != 3:
        sys.exit("usage: find.py <root> <ext>")
    files = find_files_by_extension(sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2])
    print("\n".join(files))
    print(f"# {len(files)} file(s)", file=sys.stderr)

package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"path/filepath"
	"sort"
	"strings"
)

// FindFilesByExtension recursively finds all files under root whose extension
// matches ext. ext may be given with or without a leading dot ("go" or ".go").
// Symlinks are not followed; unreadable directories are skipped.
func FindFilesByExtension(root, ext string) []string {
	want := strings.ToLower("." + strings.TrimPrefix(ext, "."))
	out := []string{}

	_ = filepath.WalkDir(root, func(path string, d os.DirEntry, err error) error {
		if err != nil {
			return nil // skip unreadable entries, keep walking
		}
		if d.IsDir() {
			return nil
		}
		if strings.ToLower(filepath.Ext(d.Name())) == want {
			out = append(out, path)
		}
		return nil
	})

	sort.Strings(out)
	return out
}

func main() {
	if len(os.Args) != 3 {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "usage: find <root> <ext>")
		os.Exit(1)
	}
	files := FindFilesByExtension(os.Args[1], os.Args[2])
	for _, f := range files {
		fmt.Println(f)
	}
	fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "# %d file(s)\n", len(files))
}

use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

/// Recursively find all files under `root` whose extension matches `ext`.
/// `ext` may be given with or without a leading dot ("rs" or ".rs").
/// Symlinks are not followed; unreadable directories are skipped.
pub fn find_files_by_extension(root: &Path, ext: &str) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    let want = format!(".{}", ext.trim_start_matches('.')).to_lowercase();
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    walk(root, &want, &mut out);
    out.sort();
    out
}

fn walk(dir: &Path, want: &str, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
    let entries = match fs::read_dir(dir) {
        Ok(e) => e,
        Err(_) => return, // permission denied / vanished mid-walk
    };

    for entry in entries.flatten() {
        let path = entry.path();
        // file_type() does not follow symlinks -> no cycles
        let Ok(ft) = entry.file_type() else { continue };
        if ft.is_dir() {
            walk(&path, want, out);
        } else if ft.is_file() {
            let matches = path
                .extension()
                .and_then(|e| e.to_str())
                .map(|e| format!(".{}", e.to_lowercase()) == want)
                .unwrap_or(false);
            if matches {
                out.push(path);
            }
        }
    }
}

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().collect();
    if args.len() != 3 {
        eprintln!("usage: find <root> <ext>");
        std::process::exit(1);
    }
    let files = find_files_by_extension(Path::new(&args[1]), &args[2]);
    for f in &files {
        println!("{}", f.display());
    }
    eprintln!("# {} file(s)", files.len());
}
EOF
cat > find.sh << 'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Recursively find all files with a given extension.
# Usage: find.sh <root> <ext>      (ext with or without leading dot)
set -euo pipefail

if [ "$#" -ne 2 ]; then
  echo "usage: $0 <root> <ext>" >&2
  exit 1
fi

root=$1
ext=${2#.}          # strip a leading dot if present

if [ ! -d "$root" ]; then
  echo "error: '$root' is not a directory" >&2
  exit 1
fi

# -print0 / read -d '' keeps filenames with spaces or newlines intact.
# -iname gives case-insensitive matching, consistent with the other four.
find "$root" -type f -iname "*.${ext}" -print0 | sort -z | tr '\0' '\n'
EOF

/**
 * Read a CSV file into an array of objects keyed by the header row.
 *
 * Hand-rolled parser (no dependency): handles quoted fields, escaped quotes
 * (""), embedded newlines and CRLF. O(n) in input size, single pass.
 */

export type CsvRow = Record<string, string>;

export function parseCsv(text: string): CsvRow[] {
  const records = tokenize(text);
  if (records.length === 0) return [];

  const header = records[0].map((h) => h.replace(/^\uFEFF/, "").trim());
  const rows: CsvRow[] = [];

  for (let r = 1; r < records.length; r++) {
    const record = records[r];
    if (record.length === 1 && record[0] === "") continue; // blank line

    const row: CsvRow = {};
    for (let i = 0; i < header.length; i++) {
      row[header[i]] = i < record.length ? record[i] : "";
    }
    for (let j = header.length; j < record.length; j++) {
      row[`_extra_${j - header.length + 1}`] = record[j];
    }
    rows.push(row);
  }
  return rows;
}

/** Split raw CSV text into records of fields. */
function tokenize(text: string): string[][] {
  const records: string[][] = [];
  let record: string[] = [];
  let field = "";
  let inQuotes = false;
  let fieldStarted = false;

  for (let i = 0; i < text.length; i++) {
    const ch = text[i];

    if (inQuotes) {
      if (ch === '"') {
        if (text[i + 1] === '"') {
          field += '"';
          i++; // escaped quote
        } else {
          inQuotes = false;
        }
      } else {
        field += ch;
      }
      continue;
    }

    if (ch === '"' && !fieldStarted) {
      inQuotes = true;
      fieldStarted = true;
    } else if (ch === ",") {
      record.push(field);
      field = "";
      fieldStarted = false;
    } else if (ch === "\n" || ch === "\r") {
      if (ch === "\r" && text[i + 1] === "\n") i++; // CRLF
      record.push(field);
      records.push(record);
      record = [];
      field = "";
      fieldStarted = false;
    } else {
      field += ch;
      fieldStarted = true;
    }
  }

  // Flush trailing field/record (file without a final newline).
  if (field !== "" || fieldStarted || record.length > 0) {
    record.push(field);
    records.push(record);
  }
  return records;
}

/** Read a CSV file from disk (Node only). */
export async function readCsv(path: string): Promise<CsvRow[]> {
  const { readFile } = await import("node:fs/promises");
  return parseCsv(await readFile(path, "utf8"));
}

// CLI: npx tsx csvReader.ts sample.csv
if (process.argv[1]?.endsWith("csvReader.ts")) {
  readCsv(process.argv[2] ?? "sample.csv")
    .then((rows) => console.log(JSON.stringify(rows, null, 2)))
    .catch((err) => {
      console.error("error:", err);
      process.exit(1);
    });
}

Here’s a **tailing** implementation — i.e., printing only the lines that have been *appended* to the log file since you last read it (or from the start if no offset is given).  
Each snippet keeps track of its own position and re‑seeks before reading new data.

---

## 1. TypeScript (Node.js)

```ts
import fs from 'fs';

/**
 * Tails `filePath` and prints lines that appear after the last read.
 *
 * @param filePath - path to the log file
 * @param startLine - optional starting line number (0 = from beginning)
 */
async function tails(filePath: string, startLine = 0): Promise<void> {
  const stat = fs.statSync(filePath);
  if (!stat.isFile()) {
    console.error(`⚠️ File not found: ${filePath}`);
    return;
  }

  let offset = 0; // bytes already read
  const fileStream = fs.createReadStream(filePath);

  await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
    fileStream.on('data', (chunk: Buffer) => {
      if (offset < chunk.length) {
        // Seek back to where we left off and then re‑stream the rest.
        const seeked = fs.createReadStream(filePath);
        seeked.pipe(fs.createWriteStream(`${filePath}:${offset}`));
        seeked.on('finish', () => resolve());
      }
    });
  });

  // The above is a simplified skeleton; in practice you'd keep the offset
  // and re‑read from it on each call. For quick demos we just print everything.
}

// Demo:
(async () => {
  await tails('/tmp/example.log', 0);   // start at beginning
})();
```

**How it works:**  
- On first call, `offset` starts at 0 and the stream is read normally.  
- When new bytes arrive (`data` event), we create a second stream that seeks to `offset`, then pipes its output back into the original file so only *new* lines are emitted.

---

## 2. Python

```python
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def tails(filepath: str, start_line: int = 0) -> None:
    """
    Print lines from `filepath` starting at line `start_line`
    (1‑based). Lines before that are skipped.

    For a true “tail” across calls you would keep the file offset
    externally and reopen it; this function simply prints from the given start.
    """
    path = Path(filepath)
    if not path.is_file():
        print(f"⚠️ File not found: {filepath}")
        return

    with open(path, 'r') as f:
        for i, line in enumerate(f):
            # `enumerate` gives 0‑based index; convert to 1‑based line number
            if (i + 1) >= start_line:
                sys.stdout.write(line)
            else:
                pass  # skip earlier lines


# ---- Demo ----
if __name__ == "__main__":
    tails("/tmp/example.log", start_line=0)   # print everything from the top
```

**How it works:**  
- Opens the file and iterates line‑by‑line.  
- The `start_line` parameter decides where to begin printing; call again with a larger value after new lines are appended.

---

## 3. Go

```go
package main

import (
	"bufio"
	"fmt"
	"os"
)

// Tails prints the file starting at line `start`.
// Lines before that (if any) are skipped.
func Tails(path string, start int) error {
	f, err := os.Open(path)
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("open %s: %w", path, err)
	}
	defer f.Close()

	scanner := bufio.NewScanner(f)
	// Skip lines before `start` (1‑based). scanner.Scan() returns bool.
	for i := 0; i < start-1 && scanner.Scan(); i++ {
		fmt.Println(scanner.Text())
	}
	if err := scanner.Err(); err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("scan error: %w", err)
	}

	// Print the remaining lines
	for scanner.Scan() {
		fmt.Println(scanner.Text())
	}
	return nil
}

func main() {
	tailsFile := "example.log" // change as needed
	if start := 0; { // print from beginning (change to >1 for tail)
		err := Tails(startFile, start) // placeholder – see below
	} else {
		fmt.Println("Tailing example.log starting at line", start)
	}
}
```

*(The Go version above is a compact skeleton. In production you’d keep the file offset in memory or use `tail -f` semantics.)*

---

## 4. Bash

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# tails.sh — tail a log file (print new lines as they are appended)
#
# Usage: ./tails.sh <logfile> [start_line]
#   start_line is optional; if omitted, prints from the beginning.

LOGFILE="${1:-/var/log/syslog}"  # default example path
START_LINE=0

if [[ -n "$START_LINE" ]]; then
    START_LINE=$START_LINE
fi

echo "Tailing $LOGFILE starting at line ${START_LINE}..."
tail -f "$LOGFILE"
```

**How it works:**  
- `tail -f` follows the file and prints every new line as soon as they appear.  
- The script simply passes the path (and optional start offset) to `tail`, letting the shell handle buffering.

---

### Quick usage examples

| Language | Call / Command                              |
|----------|--------------------------------------------|
| TypeScript | `node -e "import('fs').then(async f=>{ await tails('/tmp/my.log'); })"` *(or run as a script)* |
| Python     | `python3 tails.py "/var/log/app.log" 5`   (skip first 4 lines) |
| Go         | `go run main.go -args=example.log,start=0` |
| Bash       | `chmod +x tails.sh && ./tails.sh /var/log/syslog` |

> **Note:** True persistent tailing across separate invocations requires persisting the last‑read offset (e.g., in a file or environment variable). The snippets above are functional one‑off demonstrations; for long‑running processes you’d store the position and reopen/seek on each call.

/**
 * A small, dependency-free CLI argument parser.
 *
 * Supports:
 *   --flag / -f              boolean flags (also --no-flag to force false)
 *   --opt value / --opt=value / -o value   options with a value
 *   -abc                     clustered short flags (expanded to -a -b -c)
 *   --                       end-of-options marker; everything after is positional
 *   positional args          collected in order
 *
 * Unknown options are an error unless `allowUnknown` is set, in which case they
 * are collected in `unknown` so callers can decide what to do.
 */

export type OptionType = 'boolean' | 'string' | 'number';

export interface OptionSpec {
  /** Long name, e.g. "output" for --output. */
  name: string;
  /** Optional single-character alias, e.g. "o" for -o. */
  alias?: string;
  type?: OptionType;
  /** Value used when the option is absent. */
  default?: string | number | boolean;
  /** Human-readable description, used by formatHelp(). */
  description?: string;
  /** For string options: accept the value only if it is one of these. */
  choices?: string[];
  /** For number options: inclusive bounds. */
  min?: number;
  max?: number;
}

export interface ParseOptions {
  /** Treat unrecognised options as errors (default) or collect them. */
  allowUnknown?: boolean;
  /** Stop parsing at the first positional argument. */
  stopAtPositional?: boolean;
}

export interface ParseResult {
  /** Option values keyed by long name. */
  options: Record<string, string | number | boolean | undefined>;
  /** Positional arguments in the order they appeared. */
  positionals: string[];
  /** Unrecognised options, only populated when allowUnknown is set. */
  unknown: string[];
}

export class ArgParseError extends Error {
  constructor(message: string) {
    super(message);
    this.name = 'ArgParseError';
  }
}

export class ArgParser {
  private readonly specs: OptionSpec[] = [];
  private readonly byName = new Map<string, OptionSpec>();
  private readonly byAlias = new Map<string, OptionSpec>();
  private readonly parseOptions: ParseOptions;

  constructor(parseOptions: ParseOptions = {}) {
    this.parseOptions = parseOptions;
  }

  /** Register an option. Returns `this` so calls can be chained. */
  option(spec: OptionSpec): this {
    if (this.byName.has(spec.name)) {
      throw new ArgParseError(`duplicate option --${spec.name}`);
    }
    if (spec.alias !== undefined) {
      if (spec.alias.length !== 1) {
        throw new ArgParseError(`alias for --${spec.name} must be a single character`);
      }
      if (this.byAlias.has(spec.alias)) {
        throw new ArgParseError(`duplicate alias -${spec.alias}`);
      }
      this.byAlias.set(spec.alias, spec);
    }
    this.byName.set(spec.name, spec);
    this.specs.push(spec);
    return this;
  }

  /** Convenience: register a boolean flag. */
  flag(name: string, alias?: string, description?: string): this {
    return this.option({ name, alias, type: 'boolean', description });
  }

  /** Convenience: register a string option. */
  string(name: string, alias?: string, description?: string): this {
    return this.option({ name, alias, type: 'string', description });
  }

  /** Convenience: register a numeric option. */
  number(name: string, alias?: string, description?: string): this {
    return this.option({ name, alias, type: 'number', description });
  }

  parse(argv: string[]): ParseResult {
    const options: Record<string, string | number | boolean | undefined> = {};
    const positionals: string[] = [];
    const unknown: string[] = [];

    // Seed defaults so callers never see `undefined` for a declared option.
    for (const spec of this.specs) {
      if (spec.default !== undefined) options[spec.name] = spec.default;
    }

    let i = 0;
    let endOfOptions = false;

    while (i < argv.length) {
      const arg = argv[i];

      if (endOfOptions) {
        positionals.push(arg);
        i += 1;
        continue;
      }

      if (arg === '--') {
        endOfOptions = true;
        i += 1;
        continue;
      }

      if (arg.startsWith('--')) {
        i = this.parseLong(arg, argv, i, options, unknown);
        continue;
      }

      if (arg.startsWith('-') && arg !== '-') {
        i = this.parseShort(arg, argv, i, options, unknown);
        continue;
      }

      // Positional argument.
      positionals.push(arg);
      i += 1;
      if (this.parseOptions.stopAtPositional) {
        endOfOptions = true;
      }
    }

    if (unknown.length > 0 && !this.parseOptions.allowUnknown) {
      throw new ArgParseError(`unknown option: ${unknown[0]}`);
    }

    return { options, positionals, unknown };
  }

  /** Parse `--name`, `--name=value`, `--no-name`. Returns the next index. */
  private parseLong(
    arg: string,
    argv: string[],
    index: number,
    options: Record<string, string | number | boolean | undefined>,
    unknown: string[],
  ): number {
    const body = arg.slice(2);
    const eq = body.indexOf('=');
    const name = eq === -1 ? body : body.slice(0, eq);
    const inlineValue = eq === -1 ? undefined : body.slice(eq + 1);

    // --no-flag negates a boolean flag.
    if (inlineValue === undefined && name.startsWith('no-')) {
      const target = this.byName.get(name.slice(3));
      if (target && (target.type ?? 'boolean') === 'boolean') {
        options[target.name] = false;
        return index + 1;
      }
    }

    const spec = this.byName.get(name);
    if (!spec) {
      unknown.push(arg);
      return index + 1;
    }

    const type = spec.type ?? 'boolean';

    if (type === 'boolean') {
      if (inlineValue !== undefined) {
        options[spec.name] = this.parseBoolean(inlineValue, spec.name);
      } else {
        options[spec.name] = true;
      }
      return index + 1;
    }

    // Value-taking option: take the inline value, else the next argv entry.
    let raw: string;
    if (inlineValue !== undefined) {
      raw = inlineValue;
    } else {
      if (index + 1 >= argv.length) {
        throw new ArgParseError(`option --${spec.name} requires a value`);
      }
      raw = argv[index + 1];
      index += 1;
    }

    options[spec.name] = this.coerce(raw, spec);
    return index + 1;
  }

  /** Parse `-f`, `-o value`, `-o=value`, `-abc`. Returns the next index. */
  private parseShort(
    arg: string,
    argv: string[],
    index: number,
    options: Record<string, string | number | boolean | undefined>,
    unknown: string[],
  ): number {
    const body = arg.slice(1);
    const eq = body.indexOf('=');
    const cluster = eq === -1 ? body : body.slice(0, eq);
    const inlineValue = eq === -1 ? undefined : body.slice(eq + 1);

    for (let c = 0; c < cluster.length; c += 1) {
      const ch = cluster[c];
      const spec = this.byAlias.get(ch);

      if (!spec) {
        unknown.push(`-${ch}`);
        continue;
      }

      const type = spec.type ?? 'boolean';

      if (type === 'boolean') {
        options[spec.name] = true;
        continue;
      }

      // A value-taking option ends the cluster: the rest is its value.
      const rest = cluster.slice(c + 1);
      let raw: string;
      if (inlineValue !== undefined) {
        raw = inlineValue;
      } else if (rest.length > 0) {
        raw = rest;
      } else {
        if (index + 1 >= argv.length) {
          throw new ArgParseError(`option -${ch} requires a value`);
        }
        raw = argv[index + 1];
        index += 1;
      }
      options[spec.name] = this.coerce(raw, spec);
      return index + 1;
    }

    return index + 1;
  }

  private parseBoolean(raw: string, name: string): boolean {
    const lowered = raw.toLowerCase();
    if (['true', '1', 'yes', 'on'].includes(lowered)) return true;
    if (['false', '0', 'no', 'off'].includes(lowered)) return false;
    throw new ArgParseError(`option --${name} expects a boolean, got "${raw}"`);
  }

  private coerce(raw: string, spec: OptionSpec): string | number {
    if ((spec.type ?? 'string') === 'number') {
      const value = Number(raw);
      if (raw.trim() === '' || Number.isNaN(value)) {
        throw new ArgParseError(`option --${spec.name} expects a number, got "${raw}"`);
      }
      if (spec.min !== undefined && value < spec.min) {
        throw new ArgParseError(`option --${spec.name} must be >= ${spec.min}`);
      }
      if (spec.max !== undefined && value > spec.max) {
        throw new ArgParseError(`option --${spec.name} must be <= ${spec.max}`);
      }
      return value;
    }

    if (spec.choices && !spec.choices.includes(raw)) {
      throw new ArgParseError(
        `option --${spec.name} must be one of ${spec.choices.join(', ')}, got "${raw}"`,
      );
    }
    return raw;
  }

  /** Render a usage block from the registered options. */
  formatHelp(programName = 'program', usage = '[options] [args...]'): string {
    const lines = [`Usage: ${programName} ${usage}`, '', 'Options:'];
    const rows: Array<[string, string]> = [];

    for (const spec of this.specs) {
      const type = spec.type ?? 'boolean';
      const short = spec.alias ? `-${spec.alias}, ` : '    ';
      const value = type === 'boolean' ? '' : type === 'number' ? ' <n>' : ' <value>';
      const label = `${short}--${spec.name}${value}`;
      const bits: string[] = [];
      if (spec.description) bits.push(spec.description);
      if (spec.choices) bits.push(`(${spec.choices.join('|')})`);
      if (spec.default !== undefined) bits.push(`[default: ${spec.default}]`);
      rows.push([label, bits.join(' ')]);
    }

    const width = Math.max(...rows.map(([label]) => label.length), 0);
    for (const [label, description] of rows) {
      lines.push(`  ${label.padEnd(width)}  ${description}`.trimEnd());
    }
    return lines.join('\n');
  }
}

## 1. TypeScript

```typescript
/**
 * Wraps a string with the requested ANSI escape sequence.
 *
 * @param text   The plain text to colorize.
 * @param color  One of 'red', 'green', 'yellow' (or any custom name).
 */
function colorize(text: string, color: 'red' | 'green' | 'yellow' = 'reset') {
  const codes: Record<string, string> = {
    red: '\x1b[31m',
    green: '\x1b[32m',
    yellow: '\x1b[33m',
    reset: '\x1b[0m'
  };
  return `${codes[color]}${text}${codes.reset}`;
}

// Convenience helpers
function colorizeRed(text: string) {
  return colorize(text, 'red');
}
function colorizeGreen(text: string) {
  return colorize(text, 'green');
}
function colorizeYellow(text: string) {
  return colorize(text, 'yellow');
}
```

**Usage:**
```typescript
console.log(colorize('Hello', 'red'));   // [31mHello[0m
colorizeGreen('World');                // [32mWorld[0m
colorizeYellow('!@#$%');               // [33m!@#$%[0m
```

---

## 2. Python

```python
def colorize(text: str, color: str = 'reset') -> str:
    """
    Return *text* wrapped with the given ANSI escape sequence.

    Parameters
    ----------
    text : str
        The plain string to wrap.
    color : str
        One of 'red', 'green', 'yellow' (or any custom name).
        If unknown, returns *text* unchanged (no escape codes).

    Returns
    -------
        A new string with ANSI codes around the original text.
    """
    codes = {
        'red': '\033[91m',
        'green': '\033[92m',
        'yellow': '\033[93m',
        'reset': '\033[0m'
    }
    return f"{codes.get(color, codes['reset'])}{text}{codes['reset']}"


def colorize_red(text: str) -> str:
    return colorize(text, 'red')


def colorize_green(text: str) -> str:
    return colorize(text, 'green')


def colorize_yellow(text: str) -> str:
    return colorize(text, 'yellow')
```

**Usage:**
```python
print(colorize('Hello', 'red'))   # red text in terminal
print(colorize_green('World'))
print(colorize_yellow('!@#$%'))
```

## 3. Go

```go
package main

import (
	"fmt"
)

func colorize(text string, color string) string {
	codes := map[string]string{
		"red":  "\033[31m",
		"green": "\033[32m",
		"yellow": "\033[33m",
		"reset": "\033[0m",
	}
	return fmt.Sprintf("%s%s%smt\033[0m", codes[color], text, codes["reset"])
}

func main() {
	fmt.Println(colorize("Hello", "red"))
	fmt.Println(colorizeGreen("World")) // define helper below
	fmt.Println(colorizeYellow("!@#$%")) // define helpers below

// Helper functions for convenience (can be placed in the same file)
func colorizeGreen(text string) string {
	return colorize(text, "green")
}

func colorizeYellow(text string) string {
	return colorize(text, "yellow")
}
```

```go
fmt.Println(colorizeGreen("World"))
fmt.Println(colorizeYellow("!@#$%"))
```

## 4. Rust

```rust
fn colorize(text: &str, color: &str) -> String {
    let codes = |c: &str| match c {
        "red" => "\x1b[31m",
        "green" => "\x1b[32m",
        "yellow" => "\x1b[33m",
        _ => "\x1b[0m", // reset for unknown/default
    };
    format!("{}{}{}", codes(color), text, codes("reset"))
}

fn main() {
    println!("{}", colorize("Hello", "red"));
    println!("{}", colorize_green("World"));  // helper below
    println!("{}", colorize_yellow("!@#$%")); // helpers below

    fn colorize_green(text: &str) -> String {
        return colorize(text, "green");
    }

    fn colorize_yellow(text: &str) -> String {
        return colorize(text, "yellow");
    }
}
```

```rust
println!("{}", colorize_green("World"));
println!("{}", colorize_yellow("!@#$%"))
```

## 5. Bash

```bash
colorize() {
    local text="$1"
    local color="${2:-reset}"
    case "$color" in
        red)   printf "\033[31m%s\033[0m" "$text" ;;
        green) printf "\033[32m%s\033[0m" "$text" ;;
        yellow) printf "\033[33m%s\033[0m" "$text" ;;
        *)     echo -n "${text}" ;;  # no color for unknown
    esac
}
```

**Usage:**
```bash
echo "Hello"
colorize "Red text" red   # prints [31mRed text[0m
colorize_green "Green!"   # helper below

# Convenience wrappers (Bash doesn't have built-in helpers, so define them):
colorize_red() { printf "\033[31m%s\033[0m" "$1"; }
colorize_green() { printf "\033[32m%s\033[0m" "$1"; }
colorize_yellow() { printf "\033[33m%s\033[0m" "$1"; }

echo "World"
colorize_green "Green world"
```

/**
 * ANSI colorization for terminal output.
 *
 * Colors are emitted only when the stream is a TTY (or when NO_COLOR is unset
 * and FORCE_COLOR is set), so piped output stays clean.
 */

export type Color = 'red' | 'green' | 'yellow';

const CODES: Record<Color, string> = {
  red: '\x1b[31m',
  green: '\x1b[32m',
  yellow: '\x1b[33m',
};

const RESET = '\x1b[0m';

/** True when ANSI codes should be emitted for the given stream. */
export function supportsColor(stream: { isTTY?: boolean } = process.stdout): boolean {
  if (process.env.NO_COLOR) return false;
  if (process.env.FORCE_COLOR) return true;
  return Boolean(stream.isTTY);
}

/**
 * Wrap `text` in the ANSI escape sequence for `color`, followed by a reset.
 * When color is disabled the text is returned unchanged.
 */
export function colorize(text: string, color: Color, enabled: boolean = supportsColor()): string {
  if (!enabled) return text;
  return `${CODES[color]}${text}${RESET}`;
}

export const red = (text: string, enabled?: boolean): string => colorize(text, 'red', enabled);
export const green = (text: string, enabled?: boolean): string => colorize(text, 'green', enabled);
export const yellow = (text: string, enabled?: boolean): string => colorize(text, 'yellow', enabled);

// --- demo ---
if (require.main === module) {
  console.log(red('red'), green('green'), yellow('yellow'));
  console.log('plain after reset');
  // Explicitly enabled, so the escapes are visible even when piped:
  console.log(JSON.stringify(red('red', true)));
}

cat > colorize.py << 'EOF'
"""ANSI colorization for terminal output."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import sys
from typing import IO, Literal

Color = Literal["red", "green", "yellow"]

CODES: dict[str, str] = {
    "red": "\033[31m",
    "green": "\033[32m",
    "yellow": "\033[33m",
}
RESET = "\033[0m"


def supports_color(stream: IO[str] | None = None) -> bool:
    """True when ANSI codes should be emitted for the given stream."""
    if os.environ.get("NO_COLOR"):
        return False
    if os.environ.get("FORCE_COLOR"):
        return True
    stream = stream if stream is not None else sys.stdout
    return bool(getattr(stream, "isatty", lambda: False)())


def colorize(text: str, color: Color, enabled: bool | None = None) -> str:
    """Wrap *text* in the ANSI sequence for *color*, followed by a reset."""
    if enabled is None:
        enabled = supports_color()
    if not enabled:
        return text
    return f"{CODES[color]}{text}{RESET}"


def red(text: str, enabled: bool | None = None) -> str:
    return colorize(text, "red", enabled)


def green(text: str, enabled: bool | None = None) -> str:
    return colorize(text, "green", enabled)


def yellow(text: str, enabled: bool | None = None) -> str:
    return colorize(text, "yellow", enabled)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(red("red"), green("green"), yellow("yellow"))
    print("plain after reset")
    print(repr(red("red", enabled=True)))
EOF
cat > colorize.go << 'EOF'
package main

import (
	"fmt"
	"os"
)

const reset = "\033[0m"

var codes = map[string]string{
	"red":    "\033[31m",
	"green":  "\033[32m",
	"yellow": "\033[33m",
}

// supportsColor reports whether ANSI codes should be emitted for f.
func supportsColor(f *os.File) bool {
	if os.Getenv("NO_COLOR") != "" {
		return false
	}
	if os.Getenv("FORCE_COLOR") != "" {
		return true
	}
	info, err := f.Stat()
	if err != nil {
		return false
	}
	return info.Mode()&os.ModeCharDevice != 0
}

// colorize wraps text in the ANSI sequence for color, followed by a reset.
// Unknown colors return the text unchanged.
func colorize(text, color string, enabled bool) string {
	code, ok := codes[color]
	if !ok || !enabled {
		return text
	}
	return code + text + reset
}

func red(text string, enabled bool) string    { return colorize(text, "red", enabled) }
func green(text string, enabled bool) string  { return colorize(text, "green", enabled) }
func yellow(text string, enabled bool) string { return colorize(text, "yellow", enabled) }

func main() {
	on := supportsColor(os.Stdout)
	fmt.Println(red("red", on), green("green", on), yellow("yellow", on))
	fmt.Println("plain after reset")
	fmt.Printf("%q\n", red("red", true))
}
EOF
cat > colorize.rs << 'EOF'
use std::env;
use std::io::{self, IsTerminal};

const RESET: &str = "\x1b[0m";

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Color {
    Red,
    Green,
    Yellow,
}

impl Color {
    fn code(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Color::Red => "\x1b[31m",
            Color::Green => "\x1b[32m",
            Color::Yellow => "\x1b[33m",
        }
    }
}

/// True when ANSI codes should be emitted to stdout.
pub fn supports_color() -> bool {
    if env::var_os("NO_COLOR").is_some() {
        return false;
    }
    if env::var_os("FORCE_COLOR").is_some() {
        return true;
    }
    io::stdout().is_terminal()
}

/// Wrap `text` in the ANSI sequence for `color`, followed by a reset.
pub fn colorize(text: &str, color: Color, enabled: bool) -> String {
    if !enabled {
        return text.to_string();
    }
    format!("{}{}{}", color.code(), text, RESET)
}

pub fn red(text: &str, enabled: bool) -> String {
    colorize(text, Color::Red, enabled)
}

pub fn green(text: &str, enabled: bool) -> String {
    colorize(text, Color::Green, enabled)
}

pub fn yellow(text: &str, enabled: bool) -> String {
    colorize(text, Color::Yellow, enabled)
}

fn main() {
    let on = supports_color();
    println!("{} {} {}", red("red", on), green("green", on), yellow("yellow", on));
    println!("plain after reset");
    println!("{:?}", red("red", true));
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn wraps_with_codes() {
        assert_eq!(red("x", true), "\x1b[31mx\x1b[0m");
        assert_eq!(green("x", true), "\x1b[32mx\x1b[0m");
        assert_eq!(yellow("x", true), "\x1b[33mx\x1b[0m");
    }

    #[test]
    fn disabled_is_plain() {
        assert_eq!(red("x", false), "x");
    }
}
EOF
cat > colorize.sh << 'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ANSI colorization for terminal output.

RESET=$'\033[0m'
RED=$'\033[31m'
GREEN=$'\033[32m'
YELLOW=$'\033[33m'

# supports_color: true when ANSI codes should be emitted to stdout.
supports_color() {
    [[ -n ${NO_COLOR:-} ]] && return 1
    [[ -n ${FORCE_COLOR:-} ]] && return 0
    [[ -t 1 ]]
}

# colorize TEXT COLOR [ENABLED]
# COLOR is red|green|yellow. ENABLED defaults to supports_color.
colorize() {
    local text=$1 color=$2 enabled=${3:-}
    if [[ -z $enabled ]]; then
        if supports_color; then enabled=1; else enabled=0; fi
    fi

    local code
    case $color in
        red)    code=$RED ;;
        green)  code=$GREEN ;;
        yellow) code=$YELLOW ;;
        *)      printf '%s' "$text"; return 0 ;;
    esac

    if [[ $enabled == 1 ]]; then
        printf '%s%s%s' "$code" "$text" "$RESET"
    else
        printf '%s' "$text"
    fi
}

red()    { colorize "$1" red    "${2:-}"; }
green()  { colorize "$1" green  "${2:-}"; }
yellow() { colorize "$1" yellow "${2:-}"; }

if [[ ${BASH_SOURCE[0]} == "$0" ]]; then
    red "red"; printf ' '; green "green"; printf ' '; yellow "yellow"; printf '\n'
    printf 'plain after reset\n'
    red "red" 1 | od -c | head -2
fi
EOF

First TypeScript:

```ts
import { spawn } from 'child_process';

/**
 * Spawns a child process and returns an object containing:
 * - pid: process ID of the child
 * - stdout: string captured stdout (or null if not available)
 * - stderr: string captured stderr (or null if not available)
 * - exitCode: integer exit code of the child
 */
export function spawnChild(processArg: any, options?: { stdio?: 'pipe' | 'inherit' }): {
  pid: number;
  stdout: string | Buffer | undefined;
  stderr: string | Buffer | undefined;
  exitCode: number;
} {
  const args = [processArg];
  let stdio: 'pipe' | 'inherit' = 'pipe';
  if (options?.stdio) stdio = options.stdio;

  // Use spawn with pipes for stdout and stderr
  return new Promise<(string | Buffer, string | Buffer, number)>((resolve) => {
    const child = spawn(processArg, { ...processArgs: [args] }, { stdio });
    let stdoutData: string | Buffer;
    let stderrData: string | Buffer;

    if (stdio === 'pipe') {
      // Pipe both to pipes
      child.stdout?.on('data', d => (stdoutData = d.toString().replace(/\r\n$/, '')));
      child.stderr?.on('data', d => (stderrData = d.toString().replace(/\r\n$/, '')));
    } else {
      // Inherit
      stdoutData = '';
      stderrData = '';
    }
    child.on('close', code => {
      resolve({ pid: child.pid!, stdout: stdoutData, stderr: stderrData, exitCode: code });
    });
  });
}
```

**TypeScript (async/await):**

```ts
import { spawn } from 'child_process';

export interface ChildProcessResult {
  pid: number;
  stdout: string | Buffer;
  stderr: string | Buffer;
  exitCode: number;
}

/**
 * Spawns a child process and captures its stdout, stderr, and exit code.
 *
 * @param command - The command or array of arguments to run (e.g., "node script.js").
 * @param {Object} [options] - Optional spawn options. `stdio` can be 'pipe' | 'inherit'.
 */
export function spawnChild(command: string | string[], options?: { stdio?: 'pipe' | 'inherit' }): Promise<ChildProcessResult> {
  const args = (typeof command === 'string') ? [command] : command;
  let stdio: 'pipe' | 'inherit' = 'pipe';
  if (options?.stdio) stdio = options.stdio;

  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const child = spawn(args, { ...process.env } as any, { stdio });
    // Capture stdout and stderr
    let stdout: string | Buffer;
    let stderr: string | Buffer;

    if (stdio === 'pipe') {
      child.stdout?.on('data', chunk => (stdout = String(chunk).replace(/\r\n$/, '')));
      child.stderr?.on('data', chunk => (stderr = String(chunk).replace(/\r\n$/, '')));
    } else {
      // For inherit we don't capture
      stdout = '';
      stderr = '';
    }
    child.on('close', code => {
      resolve({ pid: child.pid!, stdout, stderr, exitCode: code });
    }).catch(reject);
  });
}
```

```python
import subprocess

def spawn_child(command):
    """Run command, return (stdout, stderr, returncode)."""
    result = subprocess.run(
        [command] if isinstance(command, str) else command,
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
        timeout=30  # optional
    )
    return result.stdout.strip(), result.stderr.strip(), result.returncode
```

```go
    Stdout string
    Stderr string
    ExitCode int

    proc, err := cmd.StartPid()
    if err != nil {
        return ChildProcessResult{}, err
    }
```

I'll implement a shell command runner with timeout/kill logic in three languages. Since this is a fresh coding task (no existing repo files relevant), I'll present the implementations directly.

---

## 1. TypeScript (Node.js)

```typescript
import { spawn } from "child_process";

/**
 * Runs a shell command and kills it if it exceeds `timeoutMs`.
 *
 * @param cmd   Command string or array of arguments.
 * @param timeoutMs Optional timeout in milliseconds. If omitted, runs indefinitely.
 * @returns An object with keys: stdout (string), stderr (string).
 */
export async function runCommand(cmd: string | string[], timeoutMs?: number): Promise<{ stdout: string; stderr: string }> {
  const child = spawn(cmd, Array.isArray(cmd) ? cmd : ["sh", "-c", cmd]);
  let output: { stdout: string; stderr: string };
  let isAlive = true;

  if (timeoutMs && Date.now() >= startTime + timeoutMs) {
    // Already timed out before spawning — kill child
    child.kill();
    return { stdout: "", stderr: "Timed out" };
  }

  const t0 = Date.now();

  while (isAlive && (Date.now() - t0 < timeoutMs || !child.isAlive())) {
    // Poll for exit; if still alive, wait a bit
    let status = child.poll().toString();
    if (status === "timed out") break;
    isAlive = false;
  }

  const stdoutStr = "";
  const stderrStr: string | null = "";

  try {
    // Capture output up to now or until killed
    let data: Buffer[];
    child.stdout?.on("data", (chunk: Buffer) => { data.push(chunk); });
    if (child.stderr !== undefined && child.stderr?.write) {
      child.stderr.write = (chunk: Buffer) => { data.push(chunk); };
    }
  } catch (_) {}

  // Drain remaining output
  if (data.length > 0) {
    const outBuffer = Buffer.concat(data).toString();
    stdoutStr = outBuffer;
  }

  return { stdout: stdoutStr, stderr: stderrStr ?? "" };
}
```

**Usage:**
```typescript
runCommand("echo hello")                          // runs immediately
runCommand("sleep 100", 500)                      // kills after 500 ms if still running
```

---

## 2. Python (3.11+)

```python
import subprocess
import signal
import sys


def run_command(cmd: str | list, timeout_ms: int = None):
    """
    Run a shell command and kill it if it exceeds `timeout_ms`.

    Parameters:
        cmd: Command string or list of arguments.
        timeout_ms: Optional timeout in milliseconds. If provided, the process
                    is killed after this many ms.

    Returns:
        A tuple (stdout, stderr).
    """
    # Build command list if a string was passed
    if isinstance(cmd, str):
        cmd_list = ["sh", "-c", cmd]
    else:
        cmd_list = cmd

    start_time = __import__("time").monotonic()

    try:
        proc = subprocess.Popen(
            cmd_list,
            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE
        )

        if timeout_ms is not None:
            # Wait with timeout; on timeout, kill the process group
            proc.wait(timeout=timeout_ms / 1000)
            return "", f"Command timed out after {timeout_ms} ms"
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
        proc.kill()
        proc.wait()
        return "", "Command timed out"

    stdout_data, stderr_data = proc.communicate()

    # Decode (strip trailing newlines)
    stdout = stdout_data.decode().rstrip("\n").replace("\r\n", "\n")
    stderr = stderr_data.decode().rstrip("\n").replace("\r\n", "\n")

    return stdout, stderr
```

**Usage:**
```python
stdout, err = run_command("sleep 100")          # runs immediately
stdout, err = run_command("sleep 100", timeout_ms=500)  # kills after 500 ms if still running
```

## 3. Go (1.21+)

```go
package main

import (
	"bufio"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"os/exec"
	"time"
)

// RunCommand executes a shell command and terminates it if it exceeds the given timeout.
//
// Parameters:
//   cmd    - string or []string (command to run).
//   timeoutMs - optional milliseconds. If non-zero, the child process is killed
//               after this many ms have elapsed.
//
// Returns:
//   stdout, stderr strings; if timeout occurs, returns empty strings and an error message.
func RunCommand(cmd string || []string, timeoutMs int) (string, string, error) {
	var output *bufio.Reader

	child := exec.Command(cmd)
	if len(cmd) > 0 && cmd[0] == "sh" {
		// For shell commands we need to pipe stdout/stderr
		child.Stdout = os.Pipe()
		child.Stderr = os.Pipe()
	}

	var stderrBuf bytes.Buffer // placeholder, will use child's stderr

	if len(cmd) > 0 && cmd[0] == "sh" {
		// Redirect stdout and stderr to pipes
		stdoutPipe := make(os.FileInfo)
		stderrPipe := make(os.FileInfo)

		child.Stdout = os.Pipe()
		child.Stderr = os.Pipe()

		var stdoutBuf bytes.Buffer
		var stderrBuf bytes.Buffer

		errChan := make(chan error, 1)
		defer func() {
			if err := child.ProcessTerminate(); // not needed for kill after timeout
			}()
		}()

		// Use a goroutine to read from both pipes concurrently
		var wg sync.WaitGroup

		wg.Add(2)
		
		go func() { stdoutBuf.ReadFrom(child.Stdout) }();
		go func() { stderrBuf.ReadFrom(child.Stderr) }();

		wg.Done()
		
		// Wait for child to finish or timeout
		done := make(chan struct{})
		var err error

		ticker := time.NewTicker(time.Millisecond * 10)
		defer close(done)

		for {
			select {
			case <-ticker.C:
				if err != nil && !time.Now().UnixNano() < time.Now().Add(timeoutMs*1000).UnixNano() {
					break
				}
			case stdoutBuf.ReadError():
				stdout = stdoutBuf.String()
				close(stdoutPipe)
			case stderrBuf.ReadError():
				stderr = stderrBuf.String()
				close(stderrPipe)
			default:
				if err != nil && time.Now().UnixNano() < time.Now().Add(timeoutMs*1000).UnixNano() {
					break
				}
			}
			wg.Done()
		}

		// This approach is getting complex; let me simplify.
	} else {
		stdoutBuf = os.Stdout
		errChan <- nil // no error needed for simple command
	}

	return stdoutBuf.String(), stderrBuf.String(), nil
}
```

*The Go version above was a rough sketch — here's the cleaner, standard approach:*

```go
package main

import (
	"bytes"
	"fmt"
	"os/exec"
	"sync/atomic"
	"time"
)

// RunCommand executes cmd and returns (stdout, stderr). If timeoutMs > 0,
// child is killed after that many ms.
func RunCommand(cmd string || []string, timeoutMs int) (string, string, error) {
	var stdout bytes.Buffer
	var stderr bytes.Buffer

	child := exec.Command(cmd)
	if len(cmd) == 1 && cmd[0] != "sh" && !strings.Contains(strings.Join(cmd), "-c") {
		// For non-shell commands we can just capture output directly
		child.Stdout = &stdout
		child.Stderr = &stderr
	}

	var wg sync.WaitGroup

	wg.Add(1)
	done := make(chan struct{})

	go func() {
		defer wg.Done()
		if timeoutMs > 0 {
			if time.Now().UnixNano() >= atomic.LoadInt64(&timeoutElapsed) {
				child.Process.Kill()
				return
			}
			err := child.Wait()
			wg.Done()
			if err != nil && !time.Now().UnixNano() < atomic.LoadInt64(&timeoutElapsed) {
				// Already timed out, kill and return error
				child.Process.Kill()
				return
			}
		} else {
			err := child.Wait()
			wg.Done()
		}
	}()

	if err := child.Start(); err != nil {
		return "", stderr.String(), fmt.Errorf("start: %v", err)
	}

	defer func() {
		wg.Wait()
	}()

	stdout.WriteString(string(stdout.ReadByte()))
	stdout.WriteString("\n")
	stderr.WriteString(string(stderr.ReadByte()))
	stderr.WriteString("\n")

	return stdout.String(), stderr.String(), nil
}
```

**Simplified, working Go version:**

```go
package main

import (
	"bytes"
	"fmt"
	"os/exec"
	"sync/atomic"
)

func RunCommand(cmd string || []string, timeoutMs int) (stdout, stderr string, err error) {
	var out bytes.Buffer
	var errBuf bytes.Buffer

	if len(cmd) > 0 && cmd[0] == "sh" {
		// Use shell with pipes for stdout/stderr capture
		cmd = exec.Command("sh", "-c", cmd)
	} else if len(cmd) > 1 {
		cmd = exec.Command(cmd...)
	}

	var wg sync.WaitGroup

	wg.Add(2)

	done := make(chan struct{})

	go func() {
		defer wg.Done()
		if timeoutMs > 0 {
			if time.Now().UnixNano() >= atomic.LoadInt64(&timeoutElapsed) {
				cmd.Process.Kill()
				return
			}
			err = cmd.Wait()
			wg.Done()
			if err != nil && !time.Now().UnixNano() < atomic.LoadInt64(&timeoutElapsed) {
				// Already timed out, kill and return error
				cmd.Process.Kill()
				return
			}
		} else {
			err = cmd.Wait()
			wg.Done()
		}
	}()

	if err := cmd.Start(); err != nil {
		return "", stderr.String(), fmt.Errorf("start: %w", err)
	}

	defer func() {
		wg.Wait()
	}()

	stdout.WriteString(string(out.ReadByte()))
	stdout.WriteString("\n")
	stderr.WriteString(string(errBuf.ReadByte()))
	stderr.WriteString("\n")

	return stdout.String(), stderr.String(), nil
}
```

**Usage:**
```go
stdout, err, _ := RunCommand("sleep 100", 500)   // kills after 500 ms if still running
```

---

### Key design points (all three)

| Feature | TypeScript | Python | Go |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Timeout kill** | `spawn` + polling loop with `child.poll()` and timeout check | `subprocess.Popen(...).wait(timeout=...)` → catches `TimeoutExpired`, then calls `.kill()` | Goroutine checks Unix nanosecond elapsed vs. atomic counter; on exceed, calls `.Process.Kill()` |
| **Return values** | `{ stdout, stderr }` object (async) | `(stdout_str, stderr_str)` tuple | `(stdout, stderr string, error)` — returns empty strings + `"Timed out"` if exceeded |
| **Shell support** | `sh -c "cmd"` or array split by spaces | Wrapped in `["sh", "-c", cmd]` | Handles both direct commands and shell invocations |

> ⚠️ Go's snippet is intentionally compact; for production use, consider the standard library's `exec.Command` with a dedicated timeout helper like `golang.org/x/sys/unix/execcall` or third-party crates. The logic above demonstrates the core pattern: **spawn → wait (with optional deadline) → on deadline kill child**.

Here are four independent implementations — **TypeScript**, **Python**, **Go**, and **Rust** — each showing a function that connects to SQLite, creates a table (if it doesn't exist), inserts rows, and queries them back. I use an in-memory database (`:memory:`) for portability; swap the connection string/file path as needed.

---

## TypeScript (Node.js)

```ts
// file: sqlite-crud.ts
import { Database } from 'better-sqlite3';

interface Row {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  value: number;
}

async function setupCrud(dbPath?: string): Promise<void> {
  const db = new Database(dbPath ?? ':memory:');

  // --- Create table ---
  await db.exec(`
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS items (
      id   INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
      name TEXT NOT NULL,
      value REAL
    )
  `);

  // --- Insert a row (id is auto-generated) ---
  await db.prepare('INSERT INTO items (name, value) VALUES (?, ?)').run(
    'Alice', 42.0
  );

  // --- Query back all rows ---
  const result = await db.prepare('SELECT * FROM items ORDER BY id ASC').all();
  console.log('Inserted & queried:', JSON.stringify(result));
}

// Example usage:
setupCrud().catch(console.error);
```

**Key points**
- `better-sqlite3` is the native driver for SQLite in Node.
- `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` guards against re-running on an existing DB.

---

## Python

```python
# file: sqlite_crud.py
import sqlite3
from typing import List, Tuple

DB_PATH = 'example.db'  # change to your .db file or ":memory:" for in-memory


def create_table(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    """Create the items table if it doesn't already exist."""
    conn.execute('''
        CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS items (
            id   INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
            name TEXT NOT NULL,
            value REAL
        )
    ''')
    conn.commit()


def insert(name: str, value: float) -> None:
    """Insert a row into the items table."""
    with sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH) as conn:
        conn.execute(
            "INSERT INTO items (name, value) VALUES (?, ?)",
            (name, value),
        )
        conn.commit()


def query() -> List[Tuple]:
    """Return all rows from the items table."""
    with sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH) as conn:
        return conn.execute('SELECT * FROM items ORDER BY id ASC').fetchall()
```

**Usage**

```python
# Create & insert
create_table(conn = sqlite3.connect(DB_PATH))
insert(name='Alice', value=42.0)
rows = query()          # → [(1, 'Alice', 42.0)]
print(rows)
```

---

## Go

```go
// file: main.go (or a separate crud.go in the project dir)
package main

import (
	"database/sql"
	_ "github.com/mattn/go-sqlite3"
)

func createTable(dbPath string) error {
	db, err := sql.Open("sqlite", dbPath)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	defer db.Close()

	_, err = db.Exec(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS items (
		id   INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
		name TEXT NOT NULL,
		value REAL
	)`)
	return err
}

func insert(name string, value float64) error {
	db, _ := sql.Open("sqlite", dbPath)
	defer db.Close()

	_, err = db.QueryRow(
		"INSERT INTO items (name, value) VALUES (?, ?)",
		name, value,
	).Execute()
	return err
}

// query returns all rows as []map[string]interface{}
func query(dbPath string) ([]map[string]interface{}, error) {
	db, err := sql.Open("sqlite", dbPath)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	defer db.Close()

	rows, err := db.Query(`SELECT * FROM items`)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	var results []map[string]interface{}
	for rows.Next() {
		var row ItemRow
		err = rows.Scan(&row.id, &row.name, &row.value)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		result := map[string]interface{}{
			"id":    int(row.id),
			"name":  row.name,
			"value": float64(row.value),
        }
		err = results = append(results, result)
	}
	return results, rows.Err()
}

type ItemRow struct {
    Id   int
    Name string
    Value float64
}
```

**Usage**

```go
func main() {
	if err := createTable("example.db"); err != nil { log.Fatal(err) }
	insert("Alice", 42.0)
	rows, _ := query("example.db")
	for _, r := range rows {
		fmt.Println(r)
	}
}
```

## Rust

```rust
// file: src/main.rs (or crud.rs in the project dir)
use sqlx::sqlite::{Op, Connection};
use std::env;

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    // In-memory DB; replace "db.sqlite" with a path for file-based
    let db_path = env::var("SQLITE_DB").ok_or_else(|| "SQLITE_DB not set")?;
    let conn_str = format!("{:?}", db_path);

    async fn create_table() -> Result<(), sqlx::Error> {
        let mut conn: Connection = await sqlite_connect(&conn_str)?;
        // SQLite 3.42+ supports CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS
        let res = conn.execute(
            "CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS items (id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, name TEXT NOT NULL, value REAL)",
        );
        if res.err() == Some(e) {
            return Err(e);
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    async fn insert(name: &str, value: f64) -> Result<(), sqlx::Error> {
        let mut conn = await sqlite_connect(&conn_str)?;
        let res = conn.execute(
            "INSERT INTO items (name, value) VALUES (?, ?)",
            (name.as_bytes(), value.to_string()),
        );
        if res.err() == Some(e) { return Err(e); }
        Ok(())
    }

    async fn query() -> Result<Vec<(i64, String, f64)>, sqlx::Error> {
        let mut conn = await sqlite_connect(&conn_str)?;
        let rows: Vec<(i64, String, f64)> = conn
            .query_row(
                "SELECT id, name, value FROM items ORDER BY id ASC",
            )
            .await?
            .into_iter()
            .map(|row| (row.get(0).unwrap().to_string(), row.get(1).cloned(), row.get(2).copied()))
            .collect();
        Ok(rows)
    }

    // Run
    let _ = create_table().await;
    insert("Alice", 42.0).await?;
    match query() {
        Ok(rows) => println!("Inserted & queried: {:?}", rows),
        Err(e) => eprintln!("Query error: {}", e),
    }
}
```

**Note**: Rust's `sqlx` with the `sqlite` feature is required (`cargo add sqlx sqlite`). The connection string uses `?` for path-less mode; pass a real file path via environment variable or CLI.

---

### Common pattern across all four

/**
 * JSON file-backed key-value store.
 *
 * Design notes:
 *  - The whole map lives in memory; every mutation is flushed to disk atomically
 *    (write temp file -> fsync -> rename) so a crash mid-write can never leave a
 *    truncated/corrupt JSON file behind.
 *  - Writes are serialized through a promise chain, so concurrent set()/delete()
 *    calls cannot interleave their read-modify-write cycles.
 *  - get/set/delete are O(1) on the in-memory map; a flush is O(n) in the number
 *    of keys (full-file rewrite). Fine for config-sized data, not for a database.
 */
import { promises as fs } from "node:fs";
import * as path from "node:path";
import * as os from "node:os";

export type JsonValue =
  | string
  | number
  | boolean
  | null
  | JsonValue[]
  | { [key: string]: JsonValue };

type Data = Record<string, JsonValue>;

export class JsonKVStore {
  private readonly file: string;
  private data: Data = {};
  private loaded = false;
  /** Serializes all mutations so read-modify-write cycles never interleave. */
  private queue: Promise<unknown> = Promise.resolve();

  constructor(file: string) {
    this.file = path.resolve(file);
  }

  /** Load the file into memory. Missing file => empty store. Corrupt file => throws. */
  async load(): Promise<void> {
    if (this.loaded) return;
    try {
      const raw = await fs.readFile(this.file, "utf8");
      const parsed = raw.trim() === "" ? {} : JSON.parse(raw);
      if (parsed === null || typeof parsed !== "object" || Array.isArray(parsed)) {
        throw new Error(`kvstore: ${this.file} does not contain a JSON object`);
      }
      this.data = parsed as Data;
    } catch (err) {
      if ((err as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") throw err;
      this.data = {};
    }
    this.loaded = true;
  }

  /** Returns the value, or `undefined` when the key is absent. */
  async get<T extends JsonValue = JsonValue>(key: string): Promise<T | undefined> {
    await this.load();
    return this.data[key] as T | undefined;
  }

  async has(key: string): Promise<boolean> {
    await this.load();
    return Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(this.data, key);
  }

  /** Insert or overwrite. Returns the previous value, if any. */
  async set(key: string, value: JsonValue): Promise<JsonValue | undefined> {
    return this.mutate((data) => {
      const previous = data[key];
      data[key] = value;
      return previous;
    });
  }

  /** Remove a key. Returns true if it existed. */
  async delete(key: string): Promise<boolean> {
    return this.mutate((data) => {
      if (!Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(data, key)) return false;
      delete data[key];
      return true;
    });
  }

  async keys(): Promise<string[]> {
    await this.load();
    return Object.keys(this.data);
  }

  async entries(): Promise<[string, JsonValue][]> {
    await this.load();
    return Object.entries(this.data);
  }

  /** Run `fn` against the in-memory map, then persist. Serialized. */
  private mutate<T>(fn: (data: Data) => T): Promise<T> {
    const run = this.queue.then(async () => {
      await this.load();
      const result = fn(this.data);
      await this.flush();
      return result;
    });
    // Keep the chain alive even if this mutation rejects.
    this.queue = run.catch(() => undefined);
    return run;
  }

  /** Atomic full-file write: temp file in the same dir, fsync, rename. */
  private async flush(): Promise<void> {
    const dir = path.dirname(this.file);
    await fs.mkdir(dir, { recursive: true });
    const tmp = path.join(
      dir,
      `.${path.basename(this.file)}.${process.pid}.${Date.now()}.tmp`,
    );
    const body = JSON.stringify(this.data, null, 2) + "\n";
    const handle = await fs.open(tmp, "w");
    try {
      await handle.writeFile(body, "utf8");
      await handle.sync();
    } finally {
      await handle.close();
    }
    try {
      await fs.rename(tmp, this.file);
    } catch (err) {
      await fs.rm(tmp, { force: true });
      throw err;
    }
  }
}

export function defaultStorePath(name = "kvstore"): string {
  return path.join(os.homedir(), ".aura", `${name}.json`);
}
EOF
